# The question, and it is the only question

> **Given this covariance and the central products it derives from, is the covariance correct?**

This is deliberately **not** an audit of the pipeline that produced it. Five previous passes audited
that pipeline and returned 6 → 4 → 6 → 9 → 14 outstanding defects, because the target was code under
active development and the surface grew every round. That approach was abandoned on 2026-08-10.

**Out of scope, explicitly:** the provenance machinery, the guards, the tests, the manifests-as-
software, whether any check is well written. If you find yourself reading `p4_lib.py` to judge the
quality of a gate, you have drifted. You are auditing a **fixed object**: symmetry, PSD and eigen
diagnostics, exact block reconstruction, mask/order consistency, marginal identities, finite
diagonal, and agreement with anything computable by an independent route.

value, recompute it and say whether it holds; a mismatch is a finding.

# The checks

1. **Symmetry** — max |C − Cᵀ| relative to matrix scale. State the number.
2. **PSD and eigen diagnostics** — full spectrum: min/max eigenvalue, condition number, count and
   magnitude of negative eigenvalues, and whether any negativity is round-off relative to λ_max.
   Judge, do not merely report.
3. **Finite diagonal** — all diagonal entries finite and non-negative at the matrix's own scale; no
   NaN or Inf anywhere.
4. **Mask / order consistency** — reported 5D support is `x5 > 0`, reported 4D support is `x4 > 0`.
   Verify `C4_projected`'s dimension equals the number of reported 4D bins REACHABLE from the
   reported 5D support under W-marginalisation. Independently determine how many are unreachable
   and what fraction of the 4D total they carry. The manifest claims 5 bins and 0.0000 %.
5. **Marginal identity** — construct the width-weighted W-marginalisation yourself from the edges,
   apply it to `x5`, and characterise the result's relationship to `x4`.
6. **Exact block reconstruction / independent route** — recompute at least one nontrivial property
   of `C4_projected` by a route different from the one that produced it. State what it establishes
   and what it does not. You choose the property and justify the choice.
7. **Scale sanity** — is the covariance magnitude consistent with the central values it accompanies
   (relative uncertainty per bin)? Flag anything unphysical.

# The 5D leg, which you CANNOT touch

The 5D candidate is **42.3 GB on NERSC scratch** and cannot be transferred. For that object only,
**author a self-contained Python script** that performs the checks you would want. I will run it
verbatim and return the raw output for your judgement in a later step.

**Because I am in the execution path for that leg, the script must make a MIS-RUN DETECTABLE FROM
ITS OUTPUT ALONE.** This is a hard requirement, not a preference — it is what converts my residual
execution risk from unmitigated to bounded, and it is the repo's standing convention
(`docs/orchestration/CONVENTION-receipt-ingredients.md`: every derived quantity ships its
ingredients; a verdict-only receipt is unfalsifiable). The script must print, at minimum:

- **sha256 of every input file it opens**, and the file sizes;
- **shapes** of every array it loads;
- **bin counts and mask counts** it derives, not just the ones it was told;
- a **recomputed mask fingerprint** (e.g. a hash over the sorted reported-bin index list) so a
  substituted or reordered input is visible;
- **two or three deliberately REDUNDANT quantities that must agree with each other** — e.g. a trace
  computed by summation versus by eigenvalue sum, or a total computed two ways — so that internal
  inconsistency in the output betrays a bad run even if every individual number looks plausible;
- enough intermediate values that truncation of the output is obvious.

Standard library + numpy + PyROOT only. No network. Read-only. Single fenced ```python block.

**State explicitly in your verdict that the 5D leg is UNAUDITED as of this pass.**

> Corrected 2026-08-06: this header previously pointed detail at `../benchmarking/findings/`, a directory
> that does not exist. An agent following that pointer hit a dead end, which is one reason findings written
> here were not reaching the agents that needed them.

> **BEN id allocation — take ids from your lane's RANGE, not from the shared maximum (set 2026-08-07).**
> Two collisions in one day (041, then 044): concurrent lanes each fetch, each see the same highest id,
> and each increment. Sequential allocation from a shared maximum does not work here. Ranges as Joseph
> assigned them: **the GBDT/P4 lane takes 060+; the PET/nd-unfolding lane takes 070+.** Read the range,
> not the maximum. Committed verifier receipts that cite a pre-renumber id are left as written —
> rewriting a receipt to match a later renumber would falsify it.
>
> **RENUMBERED 2026-08-09: BEN-077→061, 078→062, 079→063, 080→064.** I allocated four ids from the
> shared maximum instead of my lane's range, landing them inside the PET block — the exact mistake
> the paragraph above warns about, made while reading the paragraph above. Caught on a merge that
> brought in PET's BEN-081; no collision had occurred, but the ranges would have stopped meaning
> anything. **Four already-pushed commit messages (`aa220b4`, `34068d0`, `2fdf384`, and the
> stage-6/mechanism commit) cite the OLD ids and are left as written** — same convention as the
> verifier receipts above. Use this mapping when following a commit message into the ledger.
> The lesson generalises the header: reading the range rule is not the same as applying it, and the
> moment of allocation is when to re-read it, because `max(existing)+1` is what a tired agent
> computes by default.

| BEN-081 | **Hash-pinning a package file does not make its package importable; launchers must preflight the import path they actually execute.** Step-1 control array `56531057` verified the exact `omnifold_nn/omnifold/omnifold.py` digest, sourced the environment, loaded TensorFlow, then tasks 0/1 both died before training with `ModuleNotFoundError: omnifold` because `${REPO}/omnifold_nn` was never placed on `PYTHONPATH`. The pin proved the bytes existed but not that Python could resolve them. Task 2 was still pending when the error wake arrived, and the sibling annealed-LR launcher had the same defect. **Rules.** (1) Every launcher importing a repository-local package must export its package root explicitly; never assume sourcing an adjacent environment installs it. (2) Run `python -c 'import package, package.module'` after the final environment/module transition and before data loading or GPU work. (3) Preserve the failed launcher hash as evidence and issue a new launcher/namespace; do not edit history into appearing successful. (4) A pre-training import failure yields no scientific verdict regardless of how complete its input hashes are. | — | EP-2026-08-09-step1-dynamics |

| BEN-032 | **A filename-substring filter over a set defined by its DIRECTORIES silently under-protects, and reports the shortfall as success.** Step 0 of `PLAN-20260806-niter3-budget-and-J28-reroll.md` protects the throw slabs the J28 re-roll consumes. | BEN-034 | **`ControlPersist` masks NERSC certificate expiry, so cluster access dies silently mid-session hours after the credential lapsed — and the only outbound comms path dies with it.** `~/.ssh/nersc-cert.pub` is an sshproxy certificate valid `2026-08-05T06:07` → `2026-08-06T06:08:08` (~24 h). `~/.ssh/config` sets `ControlMaster auto` / `ControlPath ~/.ssh/cm/%r@%h:%p` / `ControlPersist 12h`, so every `ssh` after expiry kept succeeding over the *already-authenticated* master socket. That is a second, independent reason for the CLAUDE.md rule that durable notification must not be session-local — the session need not die for its comms to. Local work (repo edits, commits, `git push` to origin) is unaffected; the cluster checkout simply falls behind and needs a pull once access returns. | — | EP-2026-08-06-niter3-budget |
| BEN-035 | **`rc=$?` after a pipeline reports the LAST stage's status, so a diagnostic that pipes its command through `tail`/`grep` cannot detect that command failing.** Hit twice on 2026-08-06. First: an `srun` suite launch whose task-count check keyed on a banner the same command had suppressed, so the check could not fire. Second: a three-host ssh reachability probe written as `ssh … | tail -2` then `rc=$?` — it printed `ssh_rc=0` for three hosts that had all in fact hung, because `tail` exited 0 on empty input. Both cost a full diagnostic round trip and, worse, briefly asserted the opposite of the truth. **Rules.** (1) Capture the status of the command you are testing with no pipe in between (`cmd > f 2>&1; rc=$?`), then filter *reads* of `f` — the same shape BEN-026 prescribes for run logs, for the same reason. (2) `set -o pipefail` fixes the exit code but not the reasoning; prefer separating "run and record" from "filter and judge". (3) Corroborate a zero exit with evidence of *work done* (bytes produced, artifact present), because a silent success and a silent hang look identical through a pipe. Sibling trap in the same family: `grep` block-buffers when its stdout is a pipe or file, so a `ssh -v … \| grep … > f` diagnostic shows an EMPTY file while the connection is stalling — redirect the whole stream, then grep the file. **Third instance, 2026-08-07, and the sharpest:** `[ "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$(git rev-parse origin/main)" ] && echo PUSH CONFIRMED` printed CONFIRMED while the push had been **rejected** and the commit sat unapplied in a *conflicted rebase* — because during a conflicted rebase `HEAD` is checked out at the upstream, so the two hashes are equal precisely in the failure case. The check was not merely uninformative, it was **anti-correlated** with success. A confirmation must be able to distinguish success from the specific failure it is meant to catch: the working form asserts three things — no `.git/rebase-merge`/`rebase-apply`, the commit's subject is reachable from `origin/main`, and `HEAD == origin/main`. General rule for this family: before trusting a check, ask what it prints when the thing fails. **Fourth instance, 2026-08-07, and it went the other way: I inferred a SERVICE OUTAGE from one client command failing twice.** `squeue -u josephrb` returned `Unable to contact slurm controller` at 09:19Z and again at 09:49Z, and I reported a sustained Slurm outage. There was none: `scontrol ping` said `Slurmctld(primary) is UP`, `sinfo` listed partitions up, a single-job `squeue -j` worked, the wakerctl watch's `unreliable` counter was still **0** (so the tick's own `sacct` calls had all succeeded), and three immediate retries of the user-wide query succeeded 3/3. The *user-wide* query is simply heavier than a single-job one and times out intermittently under load. Note both directions of this error occurred within hours: earlier the same day an empty `squeue` with stderr discarded led me to report that every job had vanished. **Rule: never infer service state from one client command. Probe the service independently — `scontrol ping`, `sinfo`, a single-job query — and check any local state that would corroborate (here the watch's `unreliable` counter was decisive, because it records whether something else had been reaching Slurm all along).** | — | EP-2026-08-06-niter3-budget |

| BEN-039 | **A stored INPUT named like a MEASUREMENT produced a false exoneration on the campaign's headline product.** Diagnosing the 2026-08-07 nominal's fold-forward failure (ratio 0.7465 against a required R=1.1241), I read the artifact's `step1_class_ratio = 1.1240802949941018`, saw it match R to all digits, and reported to Joseph that *the classic step-1 defect is ruled out*. | BEN-019 | Repeated-measures grading (8 cards; gemini ×30 passes = 240 scores, opus ×4–6 passes): both graders are HIGHLY repeatable (within-card SD gemini 0.24 over 30 passes, opus 0.27; re-grade vs day-before mean|Δ| ≤ 0.85 with +0.4 mild drift) — so the BEN-015 rank disagreement (ρ=0.12) is systematic CALIBRATION, not sampling noise. gemini is deterministically ceiling-saturated (217/240 scores = 10; zero between-card discrimination); opus discriminates consistently (range 6–9, ordering matches verification-based survivorship). | BEN-028 | **A quiet log does not mean a dead job: on this Lustre filesystem Python block-buffers redirected stdout at 4 MiB.** `stat -c %o` on `/pscratch` returns `4194304`, and CPython sizes its buffered writer as `max(st_blksize, DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE)` — so `python3 script.py > out.log 2>&1` emits **nothing** on stdout until the process exits, while unbuffered stderr (TF init, warnings) appears immediately. The signature in every completed run here is identical: all stderr first, then one contiguous block of stdout at the end. On 2026-08-05 job `56375160` showed 7789 bytes and zero `ITERATION:` lines after 70 minutes and read as stalled; it was exactly half done and finished on time with VERDICT PASS. This is the same trap as the `salloc` lesson in `AGENTS.md` (a healthy q3 sweep was once **cancelled** because its log looked stuck), recurring through a different mechanism, which is why it needs its own entry. Correct liveness tests, in order: `sstat -j <id> --format=AveCPU` (CPU time accruing faster than wall time = working), produced artifacts, and any per-run stderr marker with a known rate. Do not judge by log growth. Fix at the source with `python3 -u` or `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` in launchers whose progress you intend to watch. | — | EP-2026-08-05-gate4-reissue |

| BEN-038 | **An L1 criterion turns per-cell VARIANCE into what reads as bias, and a predeclared rule phrased on the aggregate will confirm the wrong cause.** The D2 powered closure scores `1 - E_w[|1-r_b|]` over 285 cells. Because of the absolute value, symmetric per-cell noise is a one-sided penalty, so an unbiased-but-noisy estimator and a biased one produce the same aggregate. Decomposed on the baseline: signed mean response `E_w[r] = 0.63129` against a dilution ideal of `0.63321` — **bias −0.0019** — while the scatter penalty `E_w[|1-r|] - |1-E_w[r]| = 0.08443` accounts for **97.8%** of the whole 0.086354 gap to the ceiling, with 87 of 262 occupied bins OVERSHOOTING. `OPEN_ITEMS.md` (a) reached the scatter diagnosis first; the reusable traps are downstream of it. **Three of them, all hit in one session.** (1) Reading the per-band L1 column as undershoot: the `a_b >= 0.50` band reads 0.7943 against a 0.9704 ceiling and looks like a 17.6 pp deficit, but its signed bias is **−0.0116** and the top band actually overshoots (`E_w[r] = 1.0333`) — the L1 gap there is entirely scatter. This session wrote the wrong reading into a draft finding before the signed split caught it. (2) A predeclared decision rule stated on the aggregate: unit-testing it with a synthetic pure-bias-shift arm returned CONFIRMED for under-fitting, because a bias shift and a scatter reduction move the aggregate identically. Fixed by making the scatter term the primary target and adding a distinct `AGGREGATE_MOVED_BUT_NOT_VIA_SCATTER` verdict. (3) Diagnosing "where is the residual" by a per-cell LABEL correlated with the mechanism: `i_pparallel = 0` was retracted as an artifact, its replacement (tilt direction) is confounded with acceptance, and only stratifying by the quantity the mechanism depends on, each stratum against **its own** ceiling, is stable. **Rule.** For any absolute-value or chi-square-like figure of merit over many cells, split it into signed response and scatter BEFORE diagnosing, and predeclare on the term you actually intend to move. | CLM-010 (i) | EP-2026-08-07-d2-underfitting |
| BEN-042 | **A decomposition's "scatter penalty" is not what removing the bias would cost, and quoting it against the criterion's headroom inverts the answer twice.** Diagnosing D2 I found that `FINDING-20260806-niter4-decision.md` 2b compares its scatter penalty `E_w[|1-r|] - |1-E_w[r]| = 0.08443` — weight-NORMALISED, a fraction of the gap — against `residual_budget_abs = 0.046854`, an ABSOLUTE L1 (`0.20 x gap`). In consistent units the penalty is 2.37x *inside* the headroom, so the published "exceeds" is a unit mismatch and the conclusion it supports looks refuted. **It is not, and stopping there is the trap.** With `x_b = 1 - r_b`, the cost of a perfect-mean-response estimator is `E_w[|x - E_w[x]|]` (a weighted MAD), NOT `E_w[|x|] - |E_w[x]|`: the two differ because `|.|` is nonlinear under a shift — if every `x_b` shared a sign the penalty would be 0 while the spread was untouched. Measured, MAD/penalty = **4.38**, the honest counterfactual is recovery **0.6302 (FAIL)**, and the dispersion EXCEEDS the headroom by 1.85x. So the conclusion holds by a route its own arithmetic did not take, and I had a written "2b is refuted on a unit error" before computing the counterfactual. **Second consequence, same root:** the penalty is a poor dispersion proxy, so a budget ladder "read on the scatter axis" through it reports a 68.3% collapse ep8->ep16 where the MAD moved 5.5% — the penalty tracks how many cells straddle `r = 1`, and 2x budget pushed 38 of 86 overshooting cells back under. **Rules.** (1) A counterfactual on a non-linear figure of merit must be RECOMPUTED under the counterfactual, never read off an additive decomposition term. (2) Before comparing two quantities from different documents, check both are normalised the same way — `_abs` in one name and a weighted mean in the other is the tell. (3) Sibling of BEN-038: that entry prescribes splitting into signed response and scatter before diagnosing, and this one is what the split's second term does and does not mean. (4) When a correction makes a load-bearing conclusion look refuted, finish the calculation before writing the refutation — the reference point, not the conclusion, was wrong. | CLM-010 (i) | EP-2026-08-07-d2-underfitting |

| BEN-044 | **An absolute tolerance inherited into a problem whose natural scale is ~1e-80 makes a gate that cannot fail — and a covariance gate is where it hides best.** Reviewing `combine_cstat_bkgsub_100rep.py` (untracked until 2026-08-07, so never read by anything but its author) found two gates in one battery that could not fire: symmetry tested `sym_err > 1e-30` **absolutely**, and PSD tested `min_eig >= -1e-9 * max(max_eig, 1.0)` where the `max(..., 1.0)` pins the tolerance at `-1e-9` absolute. | BEN-060 | **A prediction that fails for a reason unrelated to its hypothesis is the shape that gets misread as confirmation — and I produced one, then defended it with a statistic I had eyeballed.** Before re-unfolding ten endpoints I predicted byte-identity, reasoning from the only two commits that had touched the driver since the reference run: J28's flux fix (`081ae4a`) and J33's branch-binding guard (`b596b3c`). A prediction only tests its hypothesis if the predicted observable actually depends on it; before predicting, ask what ELSE would have to be true, and state those preconditions as part of the prediction. **Second, compounding error — I then defended the conclusion with a statistic I had not computed.** Reporting the deviation I called it "scattered and sign-balanced" on a positive-bin fraction of **0.4594**, offering sign symmetry as the discriminator against a coherent physics shift. Joseph pointed out that 0.4594 over 106,940 bins is **26.6σ** from 0.5 (SE 0.00153); even a 100× correlation penalty leaves 2.7σ, and reaching balance needs the effective n near 107 bins. There IS a systematic sign preference. Worse, the argument could not have done its job even if the number had been balanced, because **a coherent shift would also show a sign preference** — sign says nothing about which hypothesis is right; only MAGNITUDE does (worst per-bin 1.9e-11, integral 2.6e-14). The sign claim was withdrawn rather than patched. The bias is in fact *evidence for* the round-off reading, since a different OpenMP partition is a different DETERMINISTIC rounding path and `sqrt(N)*eps ≈ 7.0e-13` matches the pooled mean `-1.76e-13`. **Rules.** (1) State a prediction's preconditions with it; if the observable depends on assumptions the hypothesis does not cover, a match confirms nothing. (2) Never call a proportion "≈ balanced" without computing its standard error — "0.4594 ≈ 0.5" is 26σ. (3) Before offering a statistic as a discriminator, check it can distinguish the hypotheses at all; sign symmetry cannot separate round-off from a coherent shift, so it was never the right instrument. (4) When a supporting argument dies, withdraw it and re-check what the conclusion now rests on, rather than repairing the argument to preserve the conclusion — here the conclusion survived on magnitude alone and is stronger for being stated that way. | BEN-070 | **A guard written in different units from its neighbours is the signature of this whole family — and one such guard was 55 orders of magnitude too loose to ever fire.** Repo-wide sweep (`docs/orchestration/audit_gates_that_cannot_fail.py`, 624 files) after Joseph observed that BEN-043, BEN-044 and the GBDT lane's BEN-046 were the same class filed independently in one day. **The one new instance:** `p4_lib.py:219` (and its copy at `p4_validate_active_lateral_fps.py:70`) does `require(np.all(d >= -1e-30), 'negative diagonal')` on a covariance whose measured diagonal median is `3.867e-86` (min `5.5e-102`, max `8.1e-79`) — the floor is `2.586e+55x` larger, so a negative variance of `-1e-40`, itself `2.6e+45x` the median magnitude, PASSES. The author knew the idiom, used it twice, and wrote the third check in absolute units. **Rules.** (1) When auditing a validator, compare its checks against EACH OTHER — a relative symmetry check beside an absolute positivity check is a defect on sight, and the inconsistency is easier to spot than either check's correctness. The third is a filter INSIDE the sweep. Sizing the derived-from-merged class (BEN-063), my C++ pass reported five multi-line `new TParameter<long>(` writes as reads. The fix -- look back for `TParameter<` to detect a construction -- also matches `GetIngredient<TParameter<double>>`, which is the READ idiom, so it reclassified a genuine read and took the C++ ratio count from **2 to 0**. Only `new TParameter` marks a construction. **The asymmetry is the whole lesson:** a too-narrow corpus at least tends to under-report suspiciously, whereas a filter that removes true positives emits a confident "C++ is clean" for a class being sized for the first time, and nothing downstream distinguishes that from having looked. **Rule:** every exclusion filter in a sweep needs its own positive control -- assert that a KNOWN instance survives the filter, not merely that the sweep runs. And when tightening a filter drops the hit count, treat the drop as a finding to explain rather than as the fix working. | — | EP-2026-08-07-cannot-fail-sweep |
| BEN-071 | **Read a validator's checks against each other before reading any of them against the physics — and mechanise it, because the inconsistency is far easier to see than either check's correctness.** Three times in two days a guard was written in different units from its neighbours, and twice the correct scale was already in scope two lines away: BEN-044 (`compute_cstat`: symmetry absolute beside a relative PSD, on ~1e-79 entries), BEN-070 (`p4_lib`: symmetry and PSD relative, diagonal `>= -1e-30` absolute), and `p4_validate_active_lateral_fps.mat_gates` (L66 and L68 relative, L70 absolute, with `ev[-1]` computed on L65). Reviewing each check against the physics requires knowing the physics; reviewing them against each other requires only noticing that one is shaped differently, which is why it mechanises. Swept exhaustively as `audit_validator_tolerance_units.py` (AST, not regex, because `-1e-9*max(eig,1.0)` and `abs(a-b)/total < 1e-9` are similar characters in different shapes): 307 files, 3 mixers, **1 real**. **The two false positives are the finding's real refinement:** both are absolute-against-**O(1)** — an exact-equality epsilon between floats that should be bit-identical, and a synthetic unit test whose own covariance is O(1) (measured `max|C| = 2.725`). So **mixed units is a SMELL; the defect is mixed units where the data is far from O(1)**, and the tool can classify units mechanically but cannot know the scale flowing through — the same form-versus-reachability distinction that downgraded `mat_gates` from live to latent. **Rules.** (1) In any function with two or more tolerance comparisons, require them to be in the same units or carry a comment saying why not. (2) Report unit-mixing with a per-row verdict, never as a bare hit list; a table whose rows are not triaged transfers the work rather than doing it. (3) Exclude physics BARS from such a sweep by a stated magnitude cut — `recovery >= 0.80` is absolute by specification and would flag every criterion function. (4) Prefer a LIVE positive control to a synthetic one: when the real instance is repaired the control fails loudly and forces the table to be regenerated, where a synthetic control would pass forever. | CLM-012 | EP-2026-08-08-validator-units |

| BEN-074 | **`git stash` is SHARED MUTABLE STATE across concurrent agents in one checkout -- never use it for a reproduce-on-a-clean-tree check. Joseph flagged the hazard -- the GBDT lane is running in the same checkout and actively pushing, and the stash stack has no per-agent namespace. A concurrent `git stash pop`, or my own `pop` racing their `stash`, silently reassigns uncommitted work between two agents' working trees, and the failure is nearly undiagnosable afterwards: both lanes see edits they did not make and neither has a record of the move. **Why the safe forms are strictly better and not merely safer:** a WIP commit (`git commit -m wip` then `git reset --soft HEAD~1`) is per-branch and leaves a reflog entry, so the intermediate state is RECOVERABLE; a detached `git worktree` gives a genuinely independent checkout, which is also what CLAUDE.md already mandates for audit delegates -- the same isolation argument applies to my own baseline checks. `git stash` is the only one of the three whose state lives outside any branch and outside the reflog's per-branch view. **Rules.** (1) In a checkout that any other agent or session may touch, treat `git stash` as unavailable. (2) To test whether a failure is pre-existing, prefer `git worktree add` on the pre-change commit, or `git -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null stash create` (which produces a commit object WITHOUT touching the shared stack) and diff against that. (3) The generalization worth carrying: before using any git command that mutates repo-global state (stash, `gc`, `reflog expire`, index locks), ask whether the checkout is single-tenant. | BEN-062 | **A rule stated one notch too broadly gets ignored after its third false alarm — and the sharp version of trap #8 also explains the OPEN item that the blunt version had been walking past for a week.** Asked to restate `hadd` sums `TParameter<double>` as a general rule and sweep every read in the tracked corpus, I first wrote the obvious generalization: *every `TParameter` in a merged file is a sum across playlists.* Mechanically true — ROOT's `TParameter<T>::Merge` defaults to `'+'` — and **useless as a review rule**, because the sweep (`audit_tparameter_merge_semantics.py`, 62 fields over 332 files) shows only **15 fields transit a `hadd`** at all, 10 of those take the default, and **8 of the 10 are supposed to add**: POT, event counts, migration censuses. (2) State the criterion in terms of the QUANTITY's nature (extensive/intensive/flag), not the mechanism's — the mechanism is the same in the correct and incorrect cases, which is why it cannot be the discriminator. (3) After any per-field audit, re-ask the question of every DERIVED quantity; per-field review is structurally blind to it. Ask what fraction of the hits a criterion predicts will be defects; if the honest answer is "most of them are probably fine", it is not yet the criterion. (7) Corollary for reporting: when a sweep disproves the premise it was commissioned under, that goes in the FIRST line of the result, not the caveats -- the disproof is the finding. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |

| BEN-064 | **A `max`-shaped guard lets its worst bin choose the headline, and five numerically irrelevant bins hid a 62% failure.** Building the CANDIDATE without a verifier PASS was authorized to find out whether stages 4-6 have defects of their own. Stages 4 and 5 came back clean on real products (identities <= 4.6e-14; validator PASS on eleven gates). Stage 6, which had **never executed** in the lane's history, failed with `projection mutates central (max rel 1.00e+00)`. **That message is a mask.** The `1.00` comes from 5 of 4830 4D bins that receive zero from the 5D support and carry `0.0000%` of the 4D total; `build_projection_M` validates coverage 5D->4D ("high reported bin maps to non-reported low bin") but never the converse, so orphan 4D bins survive to the central check as exact zeros. Excluding them, the real result is that the 5D->4D marginal and the independent 4D unfold disagree at **median 4.43%, p90 20.8%, max 72.8%, 3009 of 4825 bins over the 3% tolerance** — while their **integrals agree to 0.56%**. **Rules.** (1) Never report a bare `max` from a guard over many bins: publish median, p90 and count-over-tolerance beside it, or a handful of degenerate elements will always own the number and conceal the distribution behind it. (2) A coverage guard on a two-sided requirement must check both directions **at the point of construction**; a one-directional check does not merely miss cases, it converts them into a misleading symptom thousands of elements downstream. (3) A gate that has never run is an untested claim, not a safeguard — and the first execution of one should be treated as an experiment whose result is information, which is exactly what this exercise bought. (4) When first contact shows a gate cannot pass, the failure to reach for is the SPECIFICATION: this gate requires the marginal to reproduce the independent 4D, which is the convention the campaign explicitly did NOT adopt on 2026-08-07 — it predates the decision and nothing forced the contradiction into the open while the stage was unreachable. Escalated, not resolved; no tolerance touched. **(5) THE MECHANISM TEST, and why a REVERSED correlation beats an absent one.** Having excluded the lateral and statistical readings, I inferred that the `(eavail,q3)` localisation reflected the added W axis; Joseph sharpened it to a causal claim (a 4D unfold mixes W-populations with different migration at the unfolding step, so the 4.4% is the 4D's bias and the marginal is the better object). **Both of its predictions failed, and the first failed backwards:** |rel| falls with a cell's W-width (Spearman -0.22; median |rel| 0.0572 at n_W=1 monotonically down to 0.0190 at n_W=5), and W-width varies MOST along `pt` (range 1.532) -- the axis where |rel| is flattest -- while `eavail`, where the signed mean swings 13 points, has the second-smallest variation (0.236). A fourth hypothesis, phase-space edge, also died (edge cells 0.0411 vs interior 0.0543). **The by-product is worth more than the test:** W turns out to be largely REDUNDANT with `(eavail,q3)` -- 69.3% of reported 4D cells span <=2 of 6 W bins and the reachable-W table is a kinematic triangle -- which is `W^2 = M^2 + 2M*Eavail - Q^2` showing up in the binning, and it means there was never much W-mixing for a 4D unfold to get wrong. **Rules.** (a) A reversed correlation is a much stronger result than a null one and should be reported as a finding, not as "the test failed": -0.22 monotone across the full range excludes the mechanism, where ~0 would only have failed to support it. (b) Prefer a mechanism test with TWO predictions where the second derives the ORIGINAL observation from a quantity not used to find it; P2 here was decisive and cheap, and it is what turned "unsupported" into "anti-correlated". (c) Four excluded mechanisms and none established is a legitimate and reportable state -- write the referee-facing text to say exactly that rather than promoting the least-refuted candidate. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |
| BEN-065 | **A spread in a per-component SCALE produces shape error only in proportion to how much the components differ in SHAPE — asserting the first and skipping that step is how a 38.90% number came to look like a threat to a 1–2% claim.** Joseph's reasoning, recorded by him as his own and generalised here because the step that was skipped is a general one. J36's per-playlist Data/MC POT ratios span 0.1707–0.2371 (`max/min−1 = 38.90%`, POT-weighted mean absolute mixture error 9.4%), and that spread is REAL and reproduces exactly from the twelve per-playlist files. The inference drawn was that it therefore threatened a quoted claim asserting sensitivity to coherent 1–2% shape differences in the flux/Muon_Energy region. **Measured, the shape effect is 0.073% in pT and 0.143% in p∥ — 14–30x below the claim, and 0.032% in the low-pT ridge the claim localises to.** The missing step: reweighting components changes SHAPE only through the components' shape DIFFERENCES, and the twelve playlists are nearly shape-identical in reco pT, so a large reweighting of them is almost pure normalisation. The residual duly appears as a +0.119% normalisation shift, not as shape — which is J36's own long-recorded 'total normalisation is NOT biased' seen from the other side, i.e. the answer was already half-written in the ledger. **Rules.** (1) Before propagating a spread in a per-component weight to an observable, ask what the observable is a function OF; a weight spread propagates to a sum through the weighted quantity's variation, and if the components are near-identical in that quantity the spread is invisible there however large it is. (2) The general form: **an error's magnitude in its own units is not its magnitude in the units of the claim it threatens**, and the conversion factor is a physical property of the system that has to be measured, not assumed to be O(1). (3) When a large error and a small tolerance sit in the same region, the cheap resolution is usually to MEASURE the propagation rather than to argue about it — this took one pass over files already on disk and closed a question that had been argued twice. (4) Cost of getting it wrong in the safe direction: nothing, one measurement. In the unsafe direction: a published sentence withdrawn on an unquantified premise. Measure first. **Provenance note: this is the third mechanism proposed from the directing side and refuted in this lane** (lateral-replacement, W-mixing, mixture-error-threatens-983), against zero confirmed; Joseph's own instruction is to weight accordingly and keep TESTING proposed mechanisms rather than implementing them. Recording the base rate is the point — a proposer with a 0/3 record is still worth listening to, because each refutation cost one bounded measurement and two of the three produced better results than a confirmation would have. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |

# FINDINGS ledger — benchmarking / orchestration findings

Each finding has a `cross_stream` pointer to the physics claim(s) it produced (in `CLAIMS.md`). Long-form
detail lives in sibling `FINDING-<YYYYMMDD>-<slug>.md` files **in this directory** — indexed below.

> Corrected 2026-08-06: this header previously pointed detail at `../benchmarking/findings/`, a directory
> that does not exist. An agent following that pointer hit a dead end, which is one reason findings written
> here were not reaching the agents that needed them.

> **BEN id allocation — take ids from your lane's RANGE, not from the shared maximum (set 2026-08-07).**
> Two collisions in one day (041, then 044): concurrent lanes each fetch, each see the same highest id,
> and each increment. Sequential allocation from a shared maximum does not work here. Ranges as Joseph
> assigned them: **the GBDT/P4 lane takes 060+; the PET/nd-unfolding lane takes 070+.** Read the range,
> not the maximum. Committed verifier receipts that cite a pre-renumber id are left as written —
> rewriting a receipt to match a later renumber would falsify it.
>
> **RENUMBERED 2026-08-09: BEN-077→061, 078→062, 079→063, 080→064.** I allocated four ids from the
> shared maximum instead of my lane's range, landing them inside the PET block — the exact mistake
> the paragraph above warns about, made while reading the paragraph above. Caught on a merge that
> brought in PET's BEN-081; no collision had occurred, but the ranges would have stopped meaning
> anything. **Four already-pushed commit messages (`aa220b4`, `34068d0`, `2fdf384`, and the
> stage-6/mechanism commit) cite the OLD ids and are left as written** — same convention as the
> verifier receipts above. Use this mapping when following a commit message into the ledger.
> The lesson generalises the header: reading the range rule is not the same as applying it, and the
> moment of allocation is when to re-read it, because `max(existing)+1` is what a tired agent
> computes by default.

cally, which wrongly suggests an outage). **Renewal is interactive** (password + MFA via `sshproxy.sh`), so an autonomous session cannot fix it and must escalate. **What this break
s on this campaign specifically:** `send_channel_mail.py` runs *on the cluster*, so the only working outbound path to Joseph vanishes at the same instant — the failure takes out bo
th the work channel and the channel you would use to report it. **The mitigation that worked:** the `wakerctl` watch armed earlier that day is a Slurm cron job living on the cluste
r, so it is unaffected by the loss of the *local* session's access and still mails the nominal's verdict. That is a second, independent reason for the CLAUDE.md rule that durable n
otification must not be session-local — the session need not die for its comms to. Local work (repo edits, commits, `git push` to origin) is unaffected; the cluster checkout simply
 falls behind and needs a pull once access returns. | — | EP-2026-08-06-niter3-budget |
| BEN-035 | **`rc=$?` after a pipeline reports the LAST stage's status, so a diagnostic that pipes its command through `tail`/`grep` cannot detect that command failing.** Hit twice
 on 2026-08-06. First: an `srun` suite launch whose task-count check keyed on a banner the same command had suppressed, so the check could not fire. Second: a three-host ssh reacha
bility probe written as `ssh … | tail -2` then `rc=$?` — it printed `ssh_rc=0` for three hosts that had all in fact hung, because `tail` exited 0 on empty input. Both cost a full d
iagnostic round trip and, worse, briefly asserted the opposite of the truth. **Rules.** (1) Capture the status of the command you are testing with no pipe in between (`cmd > f 2>&1
; rc=$?`), then filter *reads* of `f` — the same shape BEN-026 prescribes for run logs, for the same reason. (2) `set -o pipefail` fixes the exit code but not the reasoning; prefer
 separating "run and record" from "filter and judge". (3) Corroborate a zero exit with evidence of *work done* (bytes produced, artifact present), because a silent success and a si
lent hang look identical through a pipe. Sibling trap in the same family: `grep` block-buffers when its stdout is a pipe or file, so a `ssh -v … \| grep … > f` diagnostic shows an 
EMPTY file while the connection is stalling — redirect the whole stream, then grep the file. **Third instance, 2026-08-07, and the sharpest:** `[ "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$(git r
ev-parse origin/main)" ] && echo PUSH CONFIRMED` printed CONFIRMED while the push had been **rejected** and the commit sat unapplied in a *conflicted rebase* — because during a con
flicted rebase `HEAD` is checked out at the upstream, so the two hashes are equal precisely in the failure case. The check was not merely uninformative, it was **anti-correlated** 
with success. A confirmation must be able to distinguish success from the specific failure it is meant to catch: the working form asserts three things — no `.git/rebase-merge`/`reb
ase-apply`, the commit's subject is reachable from `origin/main`, and `HEAD == origin/main`. General rule for this family: before trusting a check, ask what it prints when the thin
g fails. **Fourth instance, 2026-08-07, and it went the other way: I inferred a SERVICE OUTAGE from one client command failing twice.** `squeue -u josephrb` returned `Unable to con
tact slurm controller` at 09:19Z and again at 09:49Z, and I reported a sustained Slurm outage. There was none: `scontrol ping` said `Slurmctld(primary) is UP`, `sinfo` listed parti
tions up, a single-job `squeue -j` worked, the wakerctl watch's `unreliable` counter was still **0** (so the tick's own `sacct` calls had all succeeded), and three immediate retrie
s of the user-wide query succeeded 3/3. The *user-wide* query is simply heavier than a single-job one and times out intermittently under load. Note both directions of this error oc
curred within hours: earlier the same day an empty `squeue` with stderr discarded led me to report that every job had vanished. **Rule: never infer service state from one client co
mmand. Probe the service independently — `scontrol ping`, `sinfo`, a single-job query — and check any local state that would corroborate (here the watch's `unreliable` counter was 
decisive, because it records whether something else had been reaching Slurm all along).** | — | EP-2026-08-06-niter3-budget |

| BEN-019 | Repeated-measures grading (8 cards; gemini ×30 passes = 240 scores, opus ×4–6 passes): both graders are HIGHLY repeatable (within-card SD gemini 0.24 over 30 passes, op
us 0.27; re-grade vs day-before mean|Δ| ≤ 0.85 with +0.4 mild drift) — so the BEN-015 rank disagreement (ρ=0.12) is systematic CALIBRATION, not sampling noise. gemini is determinis
tically ceiling-saturated (217/240 scores = 10; zero between-card discrimination); opus discriminates consistently (range 6–9, ordering matches verification-based survivorship). Im
plication: re-sampling graders adds nothing; discrimination requires forced-ranking/pairwise formats or the verification layer. | — | EP-2026-07-10-lead-vetting |

| BEN-027 | **Identifiers and counts are read from the system, never recalled or eyeballed.** Three instances in one day, same root cause: (1) another user's job `56370836` (owner 
`mphagen`) was reported as this campaign's progress because the ID came from memory instead of `squeue -u josephrb`; (2) a queue rank was quoted off a record number in the listing 
rather than counting pending jobs with strictly higher priority — the corrected value was rank 45 of 1162, not what was first reported; (3) `gpu_shared` was repeatedly called the p
artition when it is the QOS (`shared_gpu_ss11` is the partition). None changed a physics result, but each was reported to the user as fact and had to be retracted, which spends the
 credibility that makes the real findings usable. Pattern: every ID, rank, count, and queue name in a status report must be produced by a command in the same turn that reports it.

| BEN-028 | **A quiet log does not mean a dead job: on this Lustre filesystem Python block-buffers redirected stdout at 4 MiB.** `stat -c %o` on `/pscratch` returns `4194304`, and 
CPython sizes its buffered writer as `max(st_blksize, DEFAULT_BUFFER_SIZE)` — so `python3 script.py > out.log 2>&1` emits **nothing** on stdout until the process exits, while unbuf
fered stderr (TF init, warnings) appears immediately. The signature in every completed run here is identical: all stderr first, then one contiguous block of stdout at the end. On 2
026-08-05 job `56375160` showed 7789 bytes and zero `ITERATION:` lines after 70 minutes and read as stalled; it was exactly half done and finished on time with VERDICT PASS. This i
s the same trap as the `salloc` lesson in `AGENTS.md` (a healthy q3 sweep was once **cancelled** because its log looked stuck), recurring through a different mechanism, which is wh
y it needs its own entry. Correct liveness tests, in order: `sstat -j <id> --format=AveCPU` (CPU time accruing faster than wall time = working), produced artifacts, and any per-run
 stderr marker with a known rate. Do not judge by log growth. Fix at the source with `python3 -u` or `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` in launchers whose progress you intend to watch. | — | EP-

| BEN-038 | **An L1 criterion turns per-cell VARIANCE into what reads as bias, and a predeclared rule phrased on the aggregate will confirm the wrong cause.** The D2 powered closur
e scores `1 - E_w[|1-r_b|]` over 285 cells. Because of the absolute value, symmetric per-cell noise is a one-sided penalty, so an unbiased-but-noisy estimator and a biased one prod

 diagnosis first; the reusable traps are downstream of it. **Three of them, all hit in one session.** (1) Reading the per-band L1 column as undershoot: the `a_b >= 0.50` band reads
 0.7943 against a 0.9704 ceiling and looks like a 17.6 pp deficit, but its signed bias is **−0.0116** and the top band actually overshoots (`E_w[r] = 1.0333`) — the L1 gap there is
 entirely scatter. This session wrote the wrong reading into a draft finding before the signed split caught it. (2) A predeclared decision rule stated on the aggregate: unit-testin
g it with a synthetic pure-bias-shift arm returned CONFIRMED for under-fitting, because a bias shift and a scatter reduction move the aggregate identically. Fixed by making the sca
tter term the primary target and adding a distinct `AGGREGATE_MOVED_BUT_NOT_VIA_SCATTER` verdict. (3) Diagnosing "where is the residual" by a per-cell LABEL correlated with the mec
hanism: `i_pparallel = 0` was retracted as an artifact, its replacement (tilt direction) is confounded with acceptance, and only stratifying by the quantity the mechanism depends o
n, each stratum against **its own** ceiling, is stable. **Rule.** For any absolute-value or chi-square-like figure of merit over many cells, split it into signed response and scatt
er BEFORE diagnosing, and predeclare on the term you actually intend to move. | CLM-010 (i) | EP-2026-08-07-d2-underfitting |
| BEN-042 | **A decomposition's "scatter penalty" is not what removing the bias would cost, and quoting it against the criterion's headroom inverts the answer twice.** Diagnosing D
2 I found that `FINDING-20260806-niter4-decision.md` 2b compares its scatter penalty `E_w[|1-r|] - |1-E_w[r]| = 0.08443` — weight-NORMALISED, a fraction of the gap — against `resid
ual_budget_abs = 0.046854`, an ABSOLUTE L1 (`0.20 x gap`). In consistent units the penalty is 2.37x *inside* the headroom, so the published "exceeds" is a unit mismatch and the con
clusion it supports looks refuted. **It is not, and stopping there is the trap.** With `x_b = 1 - r_b`, the cost of a perfect-mean-response estimator is `E_w[|x - E_w[x]|]` (a weig
hted MAD), NOT `E_w[|x|] - |E_w[x]|`: the two differ because `|.|` is nonlinear under a shift — if every `x_b` shared a sign the penalty would be 0 while the spread was untouched. 
Measured, MAD/penalty = **4.38**, the honest counterfactual is recovery **0.6302 (FAIL)**, and the dispersion EXCEEDS the headroom by 1.85x. So the conclusion holds by a route its 
own arithmetic did not take, and I had a written "2b is refuted on a unit error" before computing the counterfactual. **Second consequence, same root:** the penalty is a poor dispe
rsion proxy, so a budget ladder "read on the scatter axis" through it reports a 68.3% collapse ep8->ep16 where the MAD moved 5.5% — the penalty tracks how many cells straddle `r = 
1`, and 2x budget pushed 38 of 86 overshooting cells back under. **Rules.** (1) A counterfactual on a non-linear figure of merit must be RECOMPUTED under the counterfactual, never 
read off an additive decomposition term. (2) Before comparing two quantities from different documents, check both are normalised the same way — `_abs` in one name and a weighted me
an in the other is the tell. (3) Sibling of BEN-038: that entry prescribes splitting into signed response and scatter before diagnosing, and this one is what the split's second ter
m does and does not mean. (4) When a correction makes a load-bearing conclusion look refuted, finish the calculation before writing the refutation — the reference point, not the co
nclusion, was wrong. | CLM-010 (i) | EP-2026-08-07-d2-underfitting |

d not have done its job even if the number had been balanced, because **a coherent shift would also show a sign preference** — sign says nothing about which hypothesis is right; on

ferent OpenMP partition is a different DETERMINISTIC rounding path and `sqrt(N)*eps ≈ 7.0e-13` matches the pooled mean `-1.76e-13`. **Rules.** (1) State a prediction's precondition
s with it; if the observable depends on assumptions the hypothesis does not cover, a match confirms nothing. (2) Never call a proportion "≈ balanced" without computing its standard
 error — "0.4594 ≈ 0.5" is 26σ. (3) Before offering a statistic as a discriminator, check it can distinguish the hypotheses at all; sign symmetry cannot separate round-off from a c
oherent shift, so it was never the right instrument. (4) When a supporting argument dies, withdraw it and re-check what the conclusion now rests on, rather than repairing the argum
ent to preserve the conclusion — here the conclusion survived on magnitude alone and is stronger for being stated that way. Sibling of BEN-042 (a correction that made a conclusion 
look refuted, where the reference point rather than the conclusion was wrong). | CLM-006 | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |

ument normalisation (BEN-042) — hold the rest. The second is where a coverage harness recording which guards have ever fired in EITHER direction would pay. **(5) THIRD GUISE, 2026-

s: a `--root` that resolved to a directory containing none of the code (examined nothing, reported "0 hits"), and comments/docstrings inflating hits. The third is a filter INSIDE t
he sweep. Sizing the derived-from-merged class (BEN-063), my C++ pass reported five multi-line `new TParameter<long>(` writes as reads. The fix -- look back for `TParameter<` to de
tect a construction -- also matches `GetIngredient<TParameter<double>>`, which is the READ idiom, so it reclassified a genuine read and took the C++ ratio count from **2 to 0**. On
ly `new TParameter` marks a construction. **The asymmetry is the whole lesson:** a too-narrow corpus at least tends to under-report suspiciously, whereas a filter that removes true
 positives emits a confident "C++ is clean" for a class being sized for the first time, and nothing downstream distinguishes that from having looked. **Rule:** every exclusion filt
er in a sweep needs its own positive control -- assert that a KNOWN instance survives the filter, not merely that the sweep runs. And when tightening a filter drops the hit count, 
treat the drop as a finding to explain rather than as the fix working. | — | EP-2026-08-07-cannot-fail-sweep |
| BEN-071 | **Read a validator's checks against each other before reading any of them against the physics — and mechanise it, because the inconsistency is far easier to see than ei

1) (measured `max|C| = 2.725`). So **mixed units is a SMELL; the defect is mixed units where the data is far from O(1)**, and the tool can classify units mechanically but cannot kn

 would flag every criterion function. (4) Prefer a LIVE positive control to a synthetic one: when the real instance is repaired the control fails loudly and forces the table to be 
regenerated, where a synthetic control would pass forever. | CLM-012 | EP-2026-08-08-validator-units |

| BEN-074 | **`git stash` is SHARED MUTABLE STATE across concurrent agents in one checkout -- never use it for a reproduce-on-a-clean-tree check. Use a throwaway WIP commit or a de

pop`, or my own `pop` racing their `stash`, silently reassigns uncommitted work between two agents' working trees, and the failure is nearly undiagnosable afterwards: both lanes se
e edits they did not make and neither has a record of the move. **Why the safe forms are strictly better and not merely safer:** a WIP commit (`git commit -m wip` then `git reset -

[1m/bin/zsh -lc "fold -w 180 docs/orchestration/FINDINGS.md | sed -n '541,720p'"[0m in /Users/josephbailey/local-research/MINERvA-OmniFold/.claude/worktrees/gbdt-closeout

-soft HEAD~1`) is per-branch and leaves a reflog entry, so the intermediate state is RECOVERABLE; a detached `git worktree` gives a genuinely independent checkout, which is also wh
at CLAUDE.md already mandates for audit delegates -- the same isolation argument applies to my own baseline checks. `git stash` is the only one of the three whose state lives outsi
de any branch and outside the reflog's per-branch view. **Rules.** (1) In a checkout that any other agent or session may touch, treat `git stash` as unavailable. (2) To test whethe

he general form would flag; if most of the hits are correct code, you have the wrong invariant, and the enumeration is what tells you. (2) State the criterion in terms of the QUANT

any per-field audit, re-ask the question of every DERIVED quantity; per-field review is structurally blind to it. (4) Name a merged field for its merged meaning: `hasTruthOnlyMisse
s` reads as a boolean at every call site and is not one, and `nPlaylistsWithTruthOnlyMisses` could not have produced the fail-closed. (5) The general form worth carrying past ROOT:
 **any time a per-file PROPERTY is stored in an object whose merge policy is a COUNT's, merging silently converts a property into a tally** — and the default policy is usually the 
count's. **(6) ADDED BY JOSEPH: treat a criterion handed to you by the person directing the work as a HYPOTHESIS, not a spec.** He proposed this sweep *with* the "every TParameter 
is a sum" criterion attached, and had not validated it; the sweep's entire yield was disproving it and replacing it with extensive-vs-intensive. Had I built the sweep to CONFIRM th
e stated criterion -- reporting "57 fields sum across playlists, as expected" -- it would have been correct, useless, and indistinguishable from a real result. **The instruction to
 run a sweep is authoritative; the criterion travelling with it is a claim the sweep exists to test.** The tell was available before any code ran: the criterion asserted something 
about ALL TParameters while both known instances were flags/constants -- the hypothesis was strictly broader than its evidence. Ask what fraction of the hits a criterion predicts w
ill be defects; if the honest answer is "most of them are probably fine", it is not yet the criterion. (7) Corollary for reporting: when a sweep disproves the premise it was commis
sioned under, that goes in the FIRST line of the result, not the caveats -- the disproof is the finding. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |

sagree at **median 4.43%, p90 20.8%, max 72.8%, 3009 of 4825 bins over the 3% tolerance** — while their **integrals agree to 0.56%**. **Rules.** (1) Never report a bare `max` from 
a guard over many bins: publish median, p90 and count-over-tolerance beside it, or a handful of degenerate elements will always own the number and conceal the distribution behind i
t. (2) A coverage guard on a two-sided requirement must check both directions **at the point of construction**; a one-directional check does not merely miss cases, it converts them
 into a misleading symptom thousands of elements downstream. (3) A gate that has never run is an untested claim, not a safeguard — and the first execution of one should be treated 
as an experiment whose result is information, which is exactly what this exercise bought. (4) When first contact shows a gate cannot pass, the failure to reach for is the SPECIFICA
TION: this gate requires the marginal to reproduce the independent 4D, which is the convention the campaign explicitly did NOT adopt on 2026-08-07 — it predates the decision and no
thing forced the contradiction into the open while the stage was unreachable. Escalated, not resolved; no tolerance touched. **(5) THE MECHANISM TEST, and why a REVERSED correlatio
n beats an absent one.** Having excluded the lateral and statistical readings, I inferred that the `(eavail,q3)` localisation reflected the added W axis; Joseph sharpened it to a c
ausal claim (a 4D unfold mixes W-populations with different migration at the unfolding step, so the 4.4% is the 4D's bias and the marginal is the better object). **Both of its pred
ictions failed, and the first failed backwards:** |rel| falls with a cell's W-width (Spearman -0.22; median |rel| 0.0572 at n_W=1 monotonically down to 0.0190 at n_W=5), and W-widt
h varies MOST along `pt` (range 1.532) -- the axis where |rel| is flattest -- while `eavail`, where the signed mean swings 13 points, has the second-smallest variation (0.236). A f

ail,q3)` -- 69.3% of reported 4D cells span <=2 of 6 W bins and the reachable-W table is a kinematic triangle -- which is `W^2 = M^2 + 2M*Eavail - Q^2` showing up in the binning, a
nd it means there was never much W-mixing for a 4D unfold to get wrong. **Rules.** (a) A reversed correlation is a much stronger result than a null one and should be reported as a 
finding, not as "the test failed": -0.22 monotone across the full range excludes the mechanism, where ~0 would only have failed to support it. (b) Prefer a mechanism test with TWO 
predictions where the second derives the ORIGINAL observation from a quantity not used to find it; P2 here was decisive and cheap, and it is what turned "unsupported" into "anti-co
rrelated". (c) Four excluded mechanisms and none established is a legitimate and reportable state -- write the referee-facing text to say exactly that rather than promoting the lea
st-refuted candidate. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbdt-closeout |

ow a 38.90% number came to look like a threat to a 1–2% claim.** Joseph's reasoning, recorded by him as his own and generalised here because the step that was skipped is a general 
one. J36's per-playlist Data/MC POT ratios span 0.1707–0.2371 (`max/min−1 = 38.90%`, POT-weighted mean absolute mixture error 9.4%), and that spread is REAL and reproduces exactly 
from the twelve per-playlist files. The inference drawn was that it therefore threatened a quoted claim asserting sensitivity to coherent 1–2% shape differences in the flux/Muon_En
ergy region. **Measured, the shape effect is 0.073% in pT and 0.143% in p∥ — 14–30x below the claim, and 0.032% in the low-pT ridge the claim localises to.** The missing step: rewe
ighting components changes SHAPE only through the components' shape DIFFERENCES, and the twelve playlists are nearly shape-identical in reco pT, so a large reweighting of them is a
lmost pure normalisation. The residual duly appears as a +0.119% normalisation shift, not as shape — which is J36's own long-recorded 'total normalisation is NOT biased' seen from 
the other side, i.e. the answer was already half-written in the ledger. **Rules.** (1) Before propagating a spread in a per-component weight to an observable, ask what the observab
le is a function OF; a weight spread propagates to a sum through the weighted quantity's variation, and if the components are near-identical in that quantity the spread is invisibl
e there however large it is. (2) The general form: **an error's magnitude in its own units is not its magnitude in the units of the claim it threatens**, and the conversion factor 
is a physical property of the system that has to be measured, not assumed to be O(1). (3) When a large error and a small tolerance sit in the same region, the cheap resolution is u
sually to MEASURE the propagation rather than to argue about it — this took one pass over files already on disk and closed a question that had been argued twice. (4) Cost of gettin
g it wrong in the safe direction: nothing, one measurement. In the unsafe direction: a published sentence withdrawn on an unquantified premise. Measure first. **Provenance note: th
is is the third mechanism proposed from the directing side and refuted in this lane** (lateral-replacement, W-mixing, mixture-error-threatens-983), against zero confirmed; Joseph's
 own instruction is to weight accordingly and keep TESTING proposed mechanisms rather than implementing them. Recording the base rate is the point — a proposer with a 0/3 record is
 still worth listening to, because each refutation cost one bounded measurement and two of the three produced better results than a confirmation would have. | — | EP-2026-08-07-gbd

The supplied 4D covariance is correct under the directly testable product-level checks. I found no numerical covariance defect. This verdict does not extend to the inaccessible 5D source covariance, which remains UNAUDITED as of this pass.

The audited NPZ was 181,048,137 bytes with SHA-256 `2a6d1d6e7d3150bf3083053a74383f2d55b84db4f599e16f175bf10a6d168bde`.

The covariance ordering is strongly consistent with the independently constructed marginal central vector: `corr(log10 σ, log10 x) = 0.9923346`; reversing the central order gives `−0.01525`, and shifting it by one row drops the correlation to `0.46794`. Exact row-label identity cannot be proven because the NPZ does not carry a covariance-row index vector.

The manifest’s `integral_ratio = 1.0055775688062398` reproduces exactly as an unweighted sum of differential-bin contents. It is not the phase-space integral ratio: including bin volumes gives `1.0011307330441666` on reachable bins. This is a metadata-label issue, not a covariance defect.

The manifest’s exact minimum-eigenvalue claim does not reproduce: it states `−3.708896069535619e-92`, while the symmetric matrix gives `−7.85375303306293e-92`. Eigenvalues deep inside this 4,562-dimensional numerical nullspace are LAPACK/backend-sensitive; both values are unambiguously round-off relative to λmax, so the PSD judgment is unchanged.

Here is the self-contained 5D script. Its output is complete only if it contains both `AUDIT5D|BEGIN` and `AUDIT5D|END` with `"status":"COMPLETE"`.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Read-only standard-p4 5D PRODUCT audit.

Standard library + NumPy + PyROOT only. It writes no files. Output is
self-identifying, hashes every data input, prints all loaded shapes, derives
its own support mask, performs redundant full-matrix identities, and ends
with an explicit COMPLETE or ABORTED sentinel.

import os

import numpy as np
import ROOT

INPUTS = {

KEYS = {

}

EDGES = {

        [0, 0.07, 0.15, 0.25, 0.33, 0.40, 0.47, 0.55,
```
